Approval Form and Second Edition

Complete line-by-line wording comparison | 22 November 2026 | Prepared for Jacob Butki

Findings124-entry change registerComplete alignmentOther material

Finding

For these two supplied files, "vastly different" is not supported as a description of the overall wording of the ten recovery chapters. Their normalized word similarity is 99.5%. They are not identical: there are short revisions throughout, a substantial opening deletion in the foreword, and an added passage about medication in Chapter Ten.

What the number means

The ten chapters contain 34,562 Approval Form word tokens and 34,655 Second Edition word tokens. 34,449 tokens match in the same sequence: 99.67% of the Approval Form tokens and 99.41% of the Second Edition tokens. Similarity is 2 x matching tokens / total tokens in both versions. This measures shared wording, not agreement in meaning or the importance of an edit.

Scope

Approval-Form.pdf (68 PDF pages), identified inside as the Basic Text Approval Form, printed November 1981; and Second-Edition.pdf (120 PDF pages), labeled Second Edition, with a 1982 copyright notice. The comparison covers the ten chapters, the parallel introductory material, and a separate inventory of non-parallel cover, administrative, and back matter. Neither supplied PDF includes a personal-stories section. No claim is made here about other scans, other printings, the Grey Book, Baby Blue, or Alive & Free.

How to read this report

A means Approval Form; B means Second Edition. Page references are PDF page numbers counted from the first page of each supplied file. L numbers identify the OCR review lines in these files, not line numbers originally printed in the book. The chapter map also gives printed page numbers. Red marks only differing Approval Form word spans; blue marks only differing Second Edition word spans. Unchanged wording stays black. Context is repeated in the change register for readability; the complete alignment then includes each normalized word token exactly once per source.

Method and limits

The page images were OCRed afresh, aligned by section and word sequence, and every first-pass discrepancy was checked against cropped source images. Obvious OCR mistakes, page counters, binding marks, and reader-added underlining were removed from the comparison. Case, punctuation, and line wrapping are ignored in the numerical metric; end-of-line word splits are joined. Some hyphenation and spacing differences remain as separate format entries. This is a wording audit, not an exhaustive punctuation, capitalization, typeface, or artwork collation. Small source spelling errors are retained where visible rather than silently modernized. Similarity percentages are therefore normalized measures, not a claim of exact character-for-character identity.

Changes that warrant closer reading

Foreword opening

A PDF p.11, L2-L8; B begins its foreword at PDF p.10

The Second Edition omits the opening quotation and the paragraph explaining the White Book outline, together with the lead-in to the historical account. The historical account and most of the remaining foreword are retained.

Medication passage

A printed pp.50-51 (PDF pp.64-65); B printed pp.96-97 (PDF pp.112-113)

The Second Edition adds roughly 100 words acknowledging circumstances such as surgery or extreme physical injury when medication may be valid, then warns about self-deception and the risk of using. That passage is absent from the Approval Form at the corresponding point. This is the largest addition in the recovery chapters.

Tradition Four

A printed p.32 (PDF p.46); B printed p.61 (PDF p.77)

Both retain the statements that service bodies are not N.A. and are services used to help recovery. The specific list item hot-lines is removed in the Second Edition. This is a narrow deletion, not removal of the whole service-structure passage.

Tradition Nine

A printed p.35 (PDF p.49); B printed pp.67-68 (PDF pp.83-84)

The commentary retains the statements that boards and committees have no power to rule, censor, decide, or dictate and that they are not part of Narcotics Anonymous. The wording of this commentary is retained after normalization.

Chapter Three

A printed pp.6-7 (PDF pp.20-21); B printed pp.11-14 (PDF pp.27-30)

The normalized wording matches throughout this chapter. This does not assert identical punctuation, capitalization, or layout.

Short wording revisions

Examples in change register

Examples include these alternatives becoming this alternative; Having our thoughts becoming Shaping our thoughts; adding vigorous before personal action; and changing love to life in Chapter Ten. Some short edits may affect emphasis even when few words change.

Complete change register

124 minimal differing word blocks. Only differing words are marked. Several entries can belong to one edited sentence.

This does not constitute a license to use. There is no safe use of drugs for us. Our bodies don’t know the difference between drugs prescribed by a physician for pain and drugs ‘prescribed by ourselves’’ to get high. R2457 | A near PDF p.64, L37No corresponding wordingB PDF p.112, L33-p.113, L5This is a program of total abstinence, however, there are times, such as in cases of health problems involving surgery and/or extreme physical injury, when medication may be valid.
